Read in your language

One selector changes notes, worked examples and revision.

Language:

1. Har step par same difference

Arithmetic progression (AP) mein first term ke baad har term, pichhle term mein ek hi fixed number add karke milta hai. Yeh common difference d hai. Positive, negative ya zero ho sakta hai. First term a hai.

100, 70, 40, 10, …: d = −30 3, 3, 3, 3, …: d = 0

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

2. Subtract karne ka order

Baad wale term se uske just pehle wala term subtract karo. List pehle se AP ho toh ek adjacent pair se d milta hai. Nayi list test karte waqt successive differences aur diya hua rule dekho; sirf ek matching pair se AP prove nahin hoti.

6, 3, 0, −3, … 3 − 6 = −3 0 − 3 = −3 −3 − 0 = −3

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-54. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

3. a aur d se list banao

General form a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, … hai. List decide karne ke liye a aur d dono chahiye. First term mein d add nahin hota; second mein ek baar hota hai.

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, … a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, …

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 52-53. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

4. Finite aur infinite AP

Finite AP mein terms ki ginti limited hoti hai aur last term hota hai. Infinite AP ka last term nahin hota. Sirf starting numbers nahin, rukne ki condition bhi padho. Source mein das monthly saving totals ₹50 se ₹500 tak finite AP hain.

50, 100, 150, …, 500: finite 1, 2, 3, 4, …: infinite

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51-52. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

5. Har pattern AP nahin

1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … ka pattern hai, lekin differences constant nahin hain. Repeated-number blocks bhi block change par AP test fail kar sakte hain. Successive differences ki ek unequal pair hi AP ko disprove kar deti hai.

1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … Differences: 0, 1, 1, 2, … AP nahin hai

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-55. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

1. हर चरण में समान अंतर

समांतर श्रेढ़ी (AP) में पहले पद के बाद हर पद, पिछले पद में एक ही निश्चित संख्या जोड़कर मिलता है। यह संख्या सार्व अंतर d है। यह धनात्मक, ऋणात्मक या शून्य हो सकती है। पहला पद a है।

100, 70, 40, 10, …: d = −30 3, 3, 3, 3, …: d = 0

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

2. घटाने का क्रम सही रखें

बाद वाले पद में से ठीक पहले वाला पद घटाएँ। यदि पहले से पता है कि सूची AP है, तो d निकालने के लिए एक आसन्न जोड़ी पर्याप्त है। नई सूची को जाँचते समय क्रमिक अंतर और बताए गए नियम देखें; केवल एक जोड़ी से AP सिद्ध नहीं होती।

6, 3, 0, −3, … 3 − 6 = −3 0 − 3 = −3 −3 − 0 = −3

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-54. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

3. a और d से पद बनाएँ

सामान्य रूप a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, … है। सूची निर्धारित करने के लिए a और d दोनों चाहिए। पहले पद में d नहीं जुड़ता; दूसरे में एक बार जुड़ता है।

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, … a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, …

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 52-53. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

4. परिमित और अपरिमित AP

परिमित AP में पदों की संख्या सीमित होती है और अंतिम पद होता है। अपरिमित AP का अंतिम पद नहीं होता। केवल शुरुआती पद नहीं, रुकने की शर्त भी पढ़ें। स्रोत में दस महीनों की कुल बचत ₹50 से ₹500 तक परिमित AP है।

50, 100, 150, …, 500: परिमित 1, 2, 3, 4, …: अपरिमित

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51-52. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

5. हर पैटर्न AP नहीं होता

1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … में पैटर्न है, लेकिन अंतर समान नहीं हैं। एक जैसे दोहराए गए समूह भी समूह बदलने पर AP की जाँच में विफल हो सकते हैं। क्रमिक अंतरों में एक असमानता ही AP को गलत सिद्ध करने के लिए पर्याप्त है।

1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … अंतर: 0, 1, 1, 2, … AP नहीं है

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-55. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

1. The same difference, not just a pattern

An arithmetic progression (AP) is a list in which each term after the first is obtained by adding the same fixed number to the previous term. That number is the common difference d. It may be positive, negative or zero. The first term is a.

100, 70, 40, 10, …: d = −30 3, 3, 3, 3, …: d = 0

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

2. Subtract in the correct order

Always calculate the later term minus the term immediately before it. In a known AP, one adjacent pair is enough to find d. When testing an unfamiliar list, compare its successive differences and the stated rule; one matching pair alone does not establish an AP.

6, 3, 0, −3, … 3 − 6 = −3 0 − 3 = −3 −3 − 0 = −3

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-54. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

3. Build the list from a and d

The general form is a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, … . Both a and d are needed to specify it. The first term has no addition of d; the second adds d once.

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, … a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, …

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 52-53. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

4. Finite and infinite APs

A finite AP has finitely many terms and a last term. An infinite AP continues without a last term. Read the stopping condition, not just the first few numbers. The source gives ten monthly saving totals, from ₹50 to ₹500, as a finite AP.

50, 100, 150, …, 500: finite 1, 2, 3, 4, …: infinite

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 51-52. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

5. A regular pattern need not be an AP

The list 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … has a pattern, but its differences are not constant. Likewise, equal-looking blocks of repeated numbers can fail the AP test when a block changes. A single unequal pair of successive differences is enough to disprove an AP.

1, 1, 2, 3, 5, … Differences: 0, 1, 1, 2, … Not an AP

NCERT jemh105, printed pp 53-55. Our explanation / हमारी व्याख्या.

Sections 5.1-5.2: patterns, the definition and a, d

The printed introduction (pp49-53) that the chapter starts from: six patterns, five lists, the definition of an AP, more examples and building an AP from a and d. Printed Figs. 5.1-5.3 are not reproduced.

5.1 ke chhe patterns: kaun AP hai?

Naya shabd: pattern = ek rule jisse agla term milta hai. Book chhe daily-life patterns deti hai aur kehti hai ki kuch mein fixed number add hota hai, kuch mein multiply, kuch mein squares aate hain. Hamara check: neeche har list ke consecutive differences dekho.

(i) Reena ki salary: 8000, 8500, 9000, ... (starting ₹8000, har saal +₹500) (ii) Ladder ki rungs (cm): 45, 43, 41, 39, 37, 35, 33, 31 (har rung 2 cm chhoti; book ka Fig. 5.1 yahan nahin) (iii) Savings scheme: ₹8000 ka 5/4 guna har 3 saal mein: 10000, 12500, 15625, 19531.25 (iv) Unit squares: 1², 2², 3², ... (Fig. 5.2 yahan nahin) (v) Money box: 100, 150, 200, 250, ... (shuru ₹100, har saal +₹50) (vi) Rabbit pairs: 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8 (Fig. 5.3 yahan nahin)

Hamara check: (i) differences 500, 500 ✓ fixed add. (ii) −2, −2, ... ✓ fixed add. (iii) 12500 − 10000 = 2500 par 15625 − 12500 = 3125, difference badalta hai; ratio 5/4 fixed hai, isliye ye multiply wala pattern hai, AP nahin. (iv) 1, 4, 9: differences 3, 5, 7, fixed nahin. (v) +50, +50 ✓. (vi) 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8: differences 0, 1, 1, 2, 3, fixed nahin. Book ke hisaab se is chapter mein wo pattern padhenge jisme agla term pichhle mein fixed number add karke milta hai.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp49-51, Section 5.1 patterns (i)-(vi) and the text after them (Figs. 5.1-5.3 not reproduced). The classification checks (differences, ratios) are ours.

5.2: paanch lists aur AP ki definition

Naya shabd: term = list ka har number. Book paanch lists deti hai aur har ek ka rule batati hai.

(i) 1, 2, 3, 4, ... : har term pichhle se 1 zyada (ii) 100, 70, 40, 10, ... : har term 30 kam (iii) −3, −2, −1, 0, ... : har term mein 1 add (iv) 3, 3, 3, 3, ... : har term mein 0 add (ya subtract) (v) −1.0, −1.5, −2.0, −2.5, ... : har term mein −0.5 add (yaani 0.5 subtract) Definition (printed): arithmetic progression (AP) wo list hai jisme pehle term ko chhod kar har term pichhle term mein ek fixed number add karke milta hai. Ye fixed number common difference hai; ye positive, negative ya zero ho sakta hai.

Hamara check: common differences (i) +1, (ii) −30, (iii) +1, (iv) 0, (v) −0.5, yaani teeno tarah ke cases: positive, negative, zero.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p51, Section 5.2 lists (i)-(v), the rules, the definition of AP and common difference. The rule restatement follows the book; the term-by-term check is ours.

AP ke aur examples (a)-(e), general form, finite aur infinite AP

Book paanch finite examples deti hai aur kehti hai "explain karo ye AP kyun hain" (exercise ke roop mein chhoda gaya). Hamara explanation: har ek mein common difference nikalo.

(a) Queue mein students ki heights (cm): 147, 148, 149, ..., 157 (b) January ka ek hafte ka minimum temperature (°C): −3.1, −3.0, −2.9, −2.8, −2.7, −2.6, −2.5 (c) ₹1000 loan ka har mahine 5% chukane ke baad balance: 950, 900, 850, ..., 50 (d) Class I se XII ke toppers ko cash prizes: 200, 250, 300, 350, ..., 750 (e) Har mahine ₹50 bachat ke 10 mahine ki total savings: 50, 100, 150, ..., 500 General form (printed): a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, ... jahan a pehla term aur d common difference Finite AP: last term hota hai, jaise (a)-(e). Infinite AP: last term nahin, jaise 5.2 ki lists (i)-(v).

Hamari common differences: (a) +1, (b) +0.1, (c) −50, (d) +50, (e) +50. Book ka point: AP jaanne ke liye pehla term a aur common difference d dono chahiye.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p52, examples (a)-(e) (the book leaves "why is each an AP" as an exercise; the common differences are ours), the general form, finite and infinite APs.

a aur d diye hon to AP banao

Idea: pehle term se shuru karo, phir baar baar d add karte jao. Printed examples: a = 6, d = 3; a = 6, d = −3; a = −7, d = −2; a = 1.0, d = 0.1; a = 0, d = 1½; a = 2, d = 0.

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, ... a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, ... a = −7, d = −2: −7, −9, −11, −13, ... a = 1.0, d = 0.1: 1.0, 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, ... a = 0, d = 1½: 0, 1½, 3, 4½, 6, ... a = 2, d = 0: 2, 2, 2, 2, ... Ulta: list di ho to a = pehla term, aur d = koi bhi term minus us se pehle wala term.

Hamara check: a = 6, d = −3 mein 6 − 3 = 3, 3 − 3 = 0, 0 − 3 = −3 ✓; a = 0, d = 1½ mein 0 + 1½ = 1½, + 1½ = 3 ✓.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp52-53, "what do we need to know about an AP" and the printed a, d examples. The list-building steps are restated; the check is ours.

5.1 के छह प्रतिरूप: कौन-सा AP है?

नया शब्द: प्रतिरूप (pattern) = वह नियम जिससे अगला पद मिलता है। पुस्तक छह दैनिक जीवन के प्रतिरूप देती है और कहती है कि कुछ में निश्चित संख्या जोड़ी जाती है, कुछ में गुणा, कुछ में वर्ग आते हैं। हमारी जाँच: नीचे हर सूची के क्रमागत अंतर देखें।

(i) रीना का वेतन: 8000, 8500, 9000, ... (आरंभिक ₹8000, हर वर्ष +₹500) (ii) सीढ़ी के डंडे (सेमी): 45, 43, 41, 39, 37, 35, 33, 31 (हर डंडा 2 सेमी छोटा; पुस्तक का चित्र 5.1 यहाँ नहीं) (iii) बचत योजना: ₹8000 का 5/4 गुना हर 3 वर्ष में: 10000, 12500, 15625, 19531.25 (iv) इकाई वर्ग: 1², 2², 3², ... (चित्र 5.2 यहाँ नहीं) (v) गुल्लक: 100, 150, 200, 250, ... (आरंभ ₹100, हर वर्ष +₹50) (vi) खरगोश के जोड़े: 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8 (चित्र 5.3 यहाँ नहीं)

हमारी जाँच: (i) अंतर 500, 500 ✓ निश्चित जोड़। (ii) −2, −2, ... ✓ निश्चित जोड़। (iii) 12500 − 10000 = 2500 पर 15625 − 12500 = 3125, अंतर बदलता है; अनुपात 5/4 निश्चित है, इसलिए यह गुणा वाला प्रतिरूप है, AP नहीं। (iv) 1, 4, 9: अंतर 3, 5, 7, निश्चित नहीं। (v) +50, +50 ✓। (vi) 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8: अंतर 0, 1, 1, 2, 3, निश्चित नहीं। पुस्तक के अनुसार इस अध्याय में वह प्रतिरूप पढ़ेंगे जिसमें अगला पद पिछले में निश्चित संख्या जोड़कर मिलता है।

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp49-51, Section 5.1 patterns (i)-(vi) and the text after them (Figs. 5.1-5.3 not reproduced). The classification checks (differences, ratios) are ours.

5.2: पाँच सूचियाँ और AP की परिभाषा

नया शब्द: पद (term) = सूची की हर संख्या। पुस्तक पाँच सूचियाँ देती है और हर एक का नियम बताती है।

(i) 1, 2, 3, 4, ... : हर पद पिछले से 1 अधिक (ii) 100, 70, 40, 10, ... : हर पद 30 कम (iii) −3, −2, −1, 0, ... : हर पद में 1 जोड़ना (iv) 3, 3, 3, 3, ... : हर पद में 0 जोड़ना (या घटाना) (v) −1.0, −1.5, −2.0, −2.5, ... : हर पद में −0.5 जोड़ना (यानी 0.5 घटाना) परिभाषा (मुद्रित): समांतर श्रेढ़ी (AP) वह सूची है जिसमें पहले पद को छोड़कर हर पद पिछले पद में एक निश्चित संख्या जोड़ने से मिलता है। यह निश्चित संख्या सार्वांतर (common difference) है; यह धनात्मक, ऋणात्मक या शून्य हो सकती है।

हमारी जाँच: सार्वांतर (i) +1, (ii) −30, (iii) +1, (iv) 0, (v) −0.5, यानी तीनों प्रकार: धनात्मक, ऋणात्मक, शून्य।

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p51, Section 5.2 lists (i)-(v), the rules, the definition of AP and common difference. The rule restatement follows the book; the term-by-term check is ours.

AP के और उदाहरण (a)-(e), व्यापक रूप, परिमित और अपरिमित AP

पुस्तक पाँच परिमित उदाहरण देती है और कहती है "समझाइए कि ये AP क्यों हैं" (अभ्यास के रूप में छोड़ा गया)। हमारी व्याख्या: हर एक का सार्वांतर निकालें।

(a) पंक्ति में छात्रों की ऊँचाइयाँ (सेमी): 147, 148, 149, ..., 157 (b) जनवरी के एक सप्ताह का न्यूनतम तापमान (°C): −3.1, −3.0, −2.9, −2.8, −2.7, −2.6, −2.5 (c) ₹1000 ऋण का हर माह 5% चुकाने के बाद शेष: 950, 900, 850, ..., 50 (d) कक्षा I से XII के टॉपरों को नकद पुरस्कार: 200, 250, 300, 350, ..., 750 (e) हर माह ₹50 बचत के 10 माह की कुल बचत: 50, 100, 150, ..., 500 व्यापक रूप (मुद्रित): a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, ... जहाँ a पहला पद और d सार्वांतर परिमित AP: अंतिम पद होता है, जैसे (a)-(e)। अपरिमित AP: अंतिम पद नहीं, जैसे 5.2 की सूचियाँ (i)-(v)।

हमारे सार्वांतर: (a) +1, (b) +0.1, (c) −50, (d) +50, (e) +50। पुस्तक का बिंदु: AP जानने के लिए पहला पद a और सार्वांतर d दोनों चाहिए।

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p52, examples (a)-(e) (the book leaves "why is each an AP" as an exercise; the common differences are ours), the general form, finite and infinite APs.

a और d दिए हों तो AP बनाएँ

विचार: पहले पद से शुरू करें, फिर बार-बार d जोड़ते जाएँ। मुद्रित उदाहरण: a = 6, d = 3; a = 6, d = −3; a = −7, d = −2; a = 1.0, d = 0.1; a = 0, d = 1½; a = 2, d = 0।

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, ... a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, ... a = −7, d = −2: −7, −9, −11, −13, ... a = 1.0, d = 0.1: 1.0, 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, ... a = 0, d = 1½: 0, 1½, 3, 4½, 6, ... a = 2, d = 0: 2, 2, 2, 2, ... उल्टा: सूची दी हो तो a = पहला पद, और d = कोई भी पद घटा उससे पहले वाला पद।

हमारी जाँच: a = 6, d = −3 में 6 − 3 = 3, 3 − 3 = 0, 0 − 3 = −3 ✓; a = 0, d = 1½ में 0 + 1½ = 1½, + 1½ = 3 ✓।

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp52-53, "what do we need to know about an AP" and the printed a, d examples. The list-building steps are restated; the check is ours.

The six patterns of 5.1: which is an AP?

New term: a pattern is a rule that gives the next term. The book gives six everyday patterns and says that in some a fixed number is added, in others a fixed number multiplies, in others squares appear. Our check: look at the consecutive differences of each list below.

(i) Reena's salary: 8000, 8500, 9000, ... (starts at ₹8000, +₹500 each year) (ii) Ladder rungs (cm): 45, 43, 41, 39, 37, 35, 33, 31 (each rung 2 cm shorter; the book's Fig. 5.1 is not shown here) (iii) Savings scheme: ₹8000 becomes 5/4 times itself every 3 years: 10000, 12500, 15625, 19531.25 (iv) Unit squares: 1², 2², 3², ... (Fig. 5.2 not shown here) (v) Money box: 100, 150, 200, 250, ... (starts at ₹100, +₹50 each year) (vi) Rabbit pairs: 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8 (Fig. 5.3 not shown here)

Our check: (i) differences 500, 500 ✓ fixed addition. (ii) −2, −2, ... ✓ fixed addition. (iii) 12500 − 10000 = 2500 but 15625 − 12500 = 3125, so the difference changes; the ratio 5/4 is fixed, so this is a multiplying pattern, not an AP. (iv) 1, 4, 9: differences 3, 5, 7, not fixed. (v) +50, +50 ✓. (vi) 1, 1, 2, 3, 5, 8: differences 0, 1, 1, 2, 3, not fixed. As the book says, this chapter studies the pattern where the next term comes from adding a fixed number to the previous one.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp49-51, Section 5.1 patterns (i)-(vi) and the text after them (Figs. 5.1-5.3 not reproduced). The classification checks (differences, ratios) are ours.

5.2: the five lists and the definition of an AP

New term: a term is each number in a list. The book gives five lists and the rule for each.

(i) 1, 2, 3, 4, ... : each term is 1 more than the one before (ii) 100, 70, 40, 10, ... : each term is 30 less (iii) −3, −2, −1, 0, ... : add 1 each time (iv) 3, 3, 3, 3, ... : add (or subtract) 0 each time (v) −1.0, −1.5, −2.0, −2.5, ... : add −0.5 each time (that is, subtract 0.5) Definition (printed): an arithmetic progression (AP) is a list in which, except for the first term, each term is the previous term plus a fixed number. That fixed number is the common difference; it can be positive, negative or zero.

Our check: the common differences are (i) +1, (ii) −30, (iii) +1, (iv) 0, (v) −0.5, so all three kinds appear: positive, negative, zero.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p51, Section 5.2 lists (i)-(v), the rules, the definition of AP and common difference. The rule restatement follows the book; the term-by-term check is ours.

More APs (a)-(e), the general form, finite and infinite APs

The book gives five finite examples and says "explain why each is an AP" (left as an exercise). Our explanation: find the common difference of each.

(a) Heights (cm) of students in a queue: 147, 148, 149, ..., 157 (b) Minimum temperatures (°C) for a January week: −3.1, −3.0, −2.9, −2.8, −2.7, −2.6, −2.5 (c) Balance of a ₹1000 loan after paying 5% each month: 950, 900, 850, ..., 50 (d) Cash prizes for toppers of Classes I to XII: 200, 250, 300, 350, ..., 750 (e) Total savings after each of 10 months at ₹50 a month: 50, 100, 150, ..., 500 General form (printed): a, a + d, a + 2d, a + 3d, ... where a is the first term and d the common difference Finite AP: has a last term, like (a)-(e). Infinite AP: no last term, like the lists (i)-(v) of 5.2.

Our common differences: (a) +1, (b) +0.1, (c) −50, (d) +50, (e) +50. The book's point: to know an AP you need both the first term a and the common difference d.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed p52, examples (a)-(e) (the book leaves "why is each an AP" as an exercise; the common differences are ours), the general form, finite and infinite APs.

Build an AP from a and d

Idea: start from the first term, then keep adding d. Printed examples: a = 6, d = 3; a = 6, d = −3; a = −7, d = −2; a = 1.0, d = 0.1; a = 0, d = 1½; a = 2, d = 0.

a = 6, d = 3: 6, 9, 12, 15, ... a = 6, d = −3: 6, 3, 0, −3, ... a = −7, d = −2: −7, −9, −11, −13, ... a = 1.0, d = 0.1: 1.0, 1.1, 1.2, 1.3, ... a = 0, d = 1½: 0, 1½, 3, 4½, 6, ... a = 2, d = 0: 2, 2, 2, 2, ... The other way round: given a list, a is the first term and d is any term minus the term before it.

Our check: for a = 6, d = −3, 6 − 3 = 3, 3 − 3 = 0, 0 − 3 = −3 ✓; for a = 0, d = 1½, 0 + 1½ = 1½, + 1½ = 3 ✓.

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, printed pp52-53, "what do we need to know about an AP" and the printed a, d examples. The list-building steps are restated; the check is ours.

Spiral ke semicircles (Fig. 5.4 ki jagah)

Book ke Fig. 5.4 ki copy nahin; hamara apna schematic diagram, scale ke bina, sirf shuruaat ke 6 semicircles. Centres A aur B baari-baari se, radii 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, ... cm.

Ek semicircle ki length = pi × r, isliye 13 semicircles ki total length = pi × (0.5 + 1.0 + ... + 6.5). Radii ka sum = 0.5 × (1 + 2 + ... + 13) = 0.5 × 91 = 45.5, to length = 45.5 × 22/7 = 143 cm. Book ke answer section mein bhi 143 cm hai.

Book ke Fig. 5.5 ki copy nahin; hamara apna diagram. Beech ki rows chhodi gayi hain (· · ·); sirf row 1, 2, 3, 15 aur 16 dikhayi hain. Row 1 (20 logs) sabse neeche hai, row 16 (5 logs) sabse upar. Har circle ek log hai.

Rows mein logs 20, 19, 18, ... hain: AP, a = 20, d = -1. Sn = 200 se 41n - n² = 400, yaani n = 16 ya 25. n = 25 par 25va row = 20 + 24 × (-1) = -4 logs, jo possible nahin, isliye n = 16 aur top row mein 20 - 15 = 5 logs. Hamari check: 16/2 × (20 + 5) = 200. Book answer: 16 rows, 5 logs.

Potato race (Fig. 5.6 ki jagah)

Aaloo: 1–10. Balti se doori: m.

Hamara schematic, scale par nahin. Pehla gap 5 m hai; har agla gap 3 m hai. Har aaloo ke liye jaana aur wapas aana hai.

Hamari jaanch: 2 × (5 + 8 + … + 32) = 370 m.

Book ke Fig. 5.6 ki copy nahin; hamara apna diagram (scale ke hisaab se nahin, bas order dikhane ke liye). Bucket se pehla potato 5 m, baaki 3 m ke gap par; 10 potatoes.

Potato k bucket se 5 + 3(k - 1) m door hai: 5, 8, 11, ..., 32 m. Har potato ke liye aana-jaana, yaani 2 × door. Total = 2 × (5 + 8 + ... + 32) = 2 × [10/2 × (5 + 32)] = 2 × 185 = 370 m. Book answer: 370 m.

Ladder ki rungs, Ex 5.4 Q3 (Fig. 5.7 ki jagah)

Book ke Fig. 5.7 ki copy nahin; hamara apna schematic diagram. 11 rungs, lengths 45, 43, ..., 25 cm, neeche se upar.

Rungs 25 cm door hain aur top-bottom ke beech 250 cm, isliye rungs = 250/25 + 1 = 11. Lengths AP: a = 45, last = 25, to wood = 11/2 × (45 + 25) = 385 cm. Hamari check: d = (25 - 45)/10 = -2, matlab har rung 2 cm chhoti. Book answer: 385 cm.

Terrace ke steps, Ex 5.4 Q5 (Fig. 5.8 ki jagah)

Book ke Fig. 5.8 ki copy nahin; hamara apna side-view diagram, 15 steps (rise 1/4 m, tread 1/2 m) ke saath. Pehla step orange mein.

Step k ki height k × 1/4 m, tread 1/2 m, length 50 m, to volume = k × 1/4 × 1/2 × 50 = k × 25/4 m³. Total = 25/4 × (1 + 2 + ... + 15) = 25/4 × 120 = 750 m³. Book answer: 750 m³.

सर्पिल के अर्धवृत्त (चित्र 5.4 की जगह)

पुस्तक के चित्र 5.4 की प्रति नहीं; हमारा अपना योजनात्मक चित्र, पैमाने के बिना, केवल शुरू के 6 अर्धवृत्त। केंद्र A और B बारी-बारी से, त्रिज्याएँ 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, ... cm।

अर्धवृत्त की लंबाई = π × r, इसलिए 13 अर्धवृत्तों की कुल लंबाई = π × (0.5 + 1.0 + ... + 6.5)। त्रिज्याओं का योग = 0.5 × 91 = 45.5, तो लंबाई = 45.5 × 22/7 = 143 cm। पुस्तक की उत्तरमाला में भी 143 cm है।

हमारे मूल चित्र (लेबल सहित), NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 मुद्रित पृ. 70-71 (प्र. 5.3 प्र.18-20, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 और 5); मुद्रित चित्र 5.4-5.8 पुनः प्रस्तुत नहीं।

पंक्तियों में लट्ठे 20, 19, 18, ... हैं: AP, a = 20, d = -1। Sn = 200 से 41n - n² = 400, यानी n = 16 या 25। n = 25 पर 25वीं पंक्ति = 20 + 24 × (-1) = -4 लट्ठे, जो संभव नहीं, इसलिए n = 16 और ऊपरी पंक्ति में 20 - 15 = 5 लट्ठे। हमारी जाँच: 16/2 × (20 + 5) = 200। पुस्तक का उत्तर: 16 पंक्तियाँ, 5 लट्ठे।

हमारे मूल चित्र (लेबल सहित), NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 मुद्रित पृ. 70-71 (प्र. 5.3 प्र.18-20, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 और 5); मुद्रित चित्र 5.4-5.8 पुनः प्रस्तुत नहीं।

आलू दौड़ (चित्र 5.6 की जगह)

आलू: 1–10. बाल्टी से दूरी: m.

हमारा सांकेतिक चित्र, पैमाने पर नहीं। पहला अंतर 5 m है; हर अगला अंतर 3 m है। हर आलू के लिए जाना और लौटना है।

हमारी जाँच: 2 × (5 + 8 + … + 32) = 370 m।

पुस्तक के चित्र 5.6 की प्रति नहीं; हमारा अपना चित्र (पैमाने के अनुसार नहीं, केवल क्रम दिखाने के लिए)। बाल्टी से पहला आलू 5 m, बाकी 3 m के अंतर पर; 10 आलू।

आलू k बाल्टी से 5 + 3(k - 1) m दूर है: 5, 8, 11, ..., 32 m। हर आलू के लिए आना-जाना, यानी 2 × दूरी। कुल = 2 × (5 + 8 + ... + 32) = 2 × [10/2 × (5 + 32)] = 2 × 185 = 370 m। पुस्तक का उत्तर: 370 m।

हमारे मूल चित्र (लेबल सहित), NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 मुद्रित पृ. 70-71 (प्र. 5.3 प्र.18-20, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 और 5); मुद्रित चित्र 5.4-5.8 पुनः प्रस्तुत नहीं।

सीढ़ी के डंडे, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 (चित्र 5.7 की जगह)

पुस्तक के चित्र 5.7 की प्रति नहीं; हमारा अपना योजनात्मक चित्र। 11 डंडे, लंबाइयाँ 45, 43, ..., 25 cm, नीचे से ऊपर।

डंडे 25 cm की दूरी पर हैं और ऊपर-नीचे के बीच 250 cm, इसलिए डंडे = 250/25 + 1 = 11। लंबाइयाँ AP: a = 45, अंतिम = 25, तो लकड़ी = 11/2 × (45 + 25) = 385 cm। हमारी जाँच: d = (25 - 45)/10 = -2, यानी हर डंडा 2 cm छोटा। पुस्तक का उत्तर: 385 cm।

हमारे मूल चित्र (लेबल सहित), NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 मुद्रित पृ. 70-71 (प्र. 5.3 प्र.18-20, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 और 5); मुद्रित चित्र 5.4-5.8 पुनः प्रस्तुत नहीं।

छत के सोपान, प्र. 5.4 प्र.5 (चित्र 5.8 की जगह)

पुस्तक के चित्र 5.8 की प्रति नहीं; हमारा अपना पार्श्व-चित्र, 15 सोपान (ऊँचाई 1/4 m, चौड़ाई 1/2 m)। पहला सोपान नारंगी में।

सोपान k की ऊँचाई k × 1/4 m, चौड़ाई 1/2 m, लंबाई 50 m, तो आयतन = k × 1/4 × 1/2 × 50 = k × 25/4 m³। कुल = 25/4 × (1 + 2 + ... + 15) = 25/4 × 120 = 750 m³। पुस्तक का उत्तर: 750 m³।

हमारे मूल चित्र (लेबल सहित), NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 मुद्रित पृ. 70-71 (प्र. 5.3 प्र.18-20, प्र. 5.4 प्र.3 और 5); मुद्रित चित्र 5.4-5.8 पुनः प्रस्तुत नहीं।

The spiral of semicircles (instead of Fig. 5.4)

Not a copy of the book's Fig. 5.4; our own schematic diagram, unscaled, showing only the first 6 semicircles. Centres A and B alternate, radii 0.5, 1.0, 1.5, ... cm.

A semicircle's length = pi × r, so 13 semicircles have total length pi × (0.5 + 1.0 + ... + 6.5). The radii add up to 0.5 × 91 = 45.5, so the length is 45.5 × 22/7 = 143 cm. The book's answer section also gives 143 cm.

Rows 4 to 14 are not drawn. Each circle is one log; row numbers count from the bottom.

Not a copy of the book's Fig. 5.5; our own diagram. The middle rows are left out (· · ·); only rows 1, 2, 3, 15 and 16 are drawn. Row 1, with 20 logs, is the bottom row; row 16, with 5 logs, is the top. Each circle is one log.

The rows hold 20, 19, 18, ... logs: an AP with a = 20, d = -1. Sn = 200 gives 41n - n² = 400, so n = 16 or 25. For n = 25 the 25th row would have 20 + 24 × (-1) = -4 logs, which is impossible, so n = 16 and the top row has 20 - 15 = 5 logs. Our check: 16/2 × (20 + 5) = 200. Book answer: 16 rows, 5 logs.

Original diagrams by us (labelled), for NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 printed pp70-71 (Ex 5.3 Q18-20, Ex 5.4 Q3 and Q5); the printed Figs 5.4-5.8 are not reproduced.

The potato race (instead of Fig. 5.6)

Potato: 1–10. Distance from bucket: m.

Our schematic, not to scale. The first gap is 5 m; every later gap is 3 m. Each potato needs an outward trip and a return trip.

Our check: 2 × (5 + 8 + … + 32) = 370 m.

Not a copy of the book's Fig. 5.6; our own diagram (not to scale, it only shows the order). The first potato is 5 m from the bucket, the others 3 m apart; 10 potatoes.

Potato k is 5 + 3(k - 1) m from the bucket: 5, 8, 11, ..., 32 m. Each potato needs a trip there and back, so 2 × distance. Total = 2 × (5 + 8 + ... + 32) = 2 × [10/2 × (5 + 32)] = 2 × 185 = 370 m. Book answer: 370 m.

Original diagrams by us (labelled), for NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 printed pp70-71 (Ex 5.3 Q18-20, Ex 5.4 Q3 and Q5); the printed Figs 5.4-5.8 are not reproduced.

Ladder rungs, Ex 5.4 Q3 (instead of Fig. 5.7)

Not a copy of the book's Fig. 5.7; our own schematic diagram. 11 rungs, lengths 45, 43, ..., 25 cm, bottom to top.

Rungs are 25 cm apart and the top and bottom rungs are 250 cm apart, so rungs = 250/25 + 1 = 11. The lengths form an AP with a = 45 and last term 25, so the wood is 11/2 × (45 + 25) = 385 cm. Our check: d = (25 - 45)/10 = -2, so each rung is 2 cm shorter. Book answer: 385 cm.

Original diagrams by us (labelled), for NCERT jemh105 Reprint 2026-27 printed pp70-71 (Ex 5.3 Q18-20, Ex 5.4 Q3 and Q5); the printed Figs 5.4-5.8 are not reproduced.

Terrace steps, Ex 5.4 Q5 (instead of Fig. 5.8)

Not a copy of the book's Fig. 5.8; our own side-view diagram of 15 steps (rise 1/4 m, tread 1/2 m). The first step is orange.

Step k has height k × 1/4 m, tread 1/2 m and length 50 m, so its volume is k × 1/4 × 1/2 × 50 = k × 25/4 m³. Total = 25/4 × (1 + 2 + ... + 15) = 25/4 × 120 = 750 m³. Book answer: 750 m³.

Printed Examples 1-2

First term, common difference and AP tests. Exercise 5.1 Q2-Q3 follows below.

Example 1: fractional terms

Source ne list ko AP diya hai. First term padho, phir correct order mein subtract karo. Neeche extra pair check hamari vyakhya hai.

3/2, 1/2, −1/2, −3/2, … a = 3/2 d = 1/2 − 3/2 = −1 Check: −1/2 − 1/2 = −1

Printed Example 1, p 54.

Example 2(i): positive difference

Saare shown differences 6 hain. Diye pattern ke hisaab se list AP hai; next two terms ke liye 6 do baar add karo.

4, 10, 16, 22, … 10 − 4 = 6 16 − 10 = 6 22 − 16 = 6 d = 6 22 + 6 = 28; 28 + 6 = 34

Printed Example 2(i), p 54.

Example 2(ii): negative difference

Negative subtract karne par sign badalta hai. Har difference −2 hai, toh −2 add karte raho, +2 nahin.

1, −1, −3, −5, … −1 − 1 = −2 −3 − (−1) = −2 −5 − (−3) = −2 d = −2 −5 + (−2) = −7 −7 + (−2) = −9

Printed Example 2(ii), p 54.

Example 2(iii): alternating terms

Pehle do differences unequal hain. Isliye list AP nahin hai; ek fixed common difference nahin milta.

−2, 2, −2, 2, −2, … 2 − (−2) = 4 −2 − 2 = −4 4 ≠ −4

Printed Example 2(iii), p 54.

Example 2(iv): repeated blocks

Pehle do differences zero hain, lekin agla 1 hai. Starting matching differences par mat ruko. List AP nahin hai.

1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3, … 1 − 1 = 0 1 − 1 = 0 2 − 1 = 1 0 ≠ 1

Printed Example 2(iv), p 55.

Hamari explanations aur translations printed pp51-55 par based hain. Questions aur conclusions Examples1-2 ke hain; Example1 ka extra check hamara hai.

उदाहरण 1: भिन्न वाले पद

स्रोत में सूची को AP बताया गया है। पहला पद पढ़ें, फिर सही क्रम में घटाएँ। नीचे दूसरी जोड़ी की अतिरिक्त जाँच हमारी व्याख्या है।

3/2, 1/2, −1/2, −3/2, … a = 3/2 d = 1/2 − 3/2 = −1 Check: −1/2 − 1/2 = −1

Printed Example 1, p 54.

उदाहरण 2(i): धनात्मक अंतर

सभी दिखाए गए अंतर 6 हैं। बताए गए पैटर्न के अनुसार यह AP है। अगले दो पदों के लिए 6 दो बार जोड़ें।

4, 10, 16, 22, … 10 − 4 = 6 16 − 10 = 6 22 − 16 = 6 d = 6 22 + 6 = 28; 28 + 6 = 34

Printed Example 2(i), p 54.

उदाहरण 2(ii): ऋणात्मक अंतर

ऋणात्मक संख्या घटाने पर चिह्न बदलता है। हर अंतर −2 है, इसलिए −2 जोड़ते जाएँ, +2 नहीं।

1, −1, −3, −5, … −1 − 1 = −2 −3 − (−1) = −2 −5 − (−3) = −2 d = −2 −5 + (−2) = −7 −7 + (−2) = −9

Printed Example 2(ii), p 54.

उदाहरण 2(iii): बारी-बारी बदलते पद

पहले दो अंतर असमान हैं। इसलिए सूची AP नहीं है; एक ही सार्व अंतर नहीं मिलता।

−2, 2, −2, 2, −2, … 2 − (−2) = 4 −2 − 2 = −4 4 ≠ −4

Printed Example 2(iii), p 54.

उदाहरण 2(iv): दोहराए गए समूह

पहले दो अंतर शून्य हैं, लेकिन अगला 1 है। केवल शुरुआती समान अंतरों पर न रुकें। यह सूची AP नहीं है।

1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3, … 1 − 1 = 0 1 − 1 = 0 2 − 1 = 1 0 ≠ 1

Printed Example 2(iv), p 55.

हमारी व्याख्याएँ और अनुवाद छपे पृष्ठ 51-55 पर आधारित हैं। प्रश्न और निष्कर्ष उदाहरण 1-2 से हैं; उदाहरण 1 की अतिरिक्त जाँच हमारी है।

Example 1: fractional terms

The source already states that this list is an AP. Read the first term, then subtract in order. The extra pair check below is our explanation.

3/2, 1/2, −1/2, −3/2, … a = 3/2 d = 1/2 − 3/2 = −1 Check: −1/2 − 1/2 = −1

Printed Example 1, p 54.

Example 2(i): a positive difference

All shown differences are 6. With the stated pattern, the list is an AP; add 6 twice to extend it.

4, 10, 16, 22, … 10 − 4 = 6 16 − 10 = 6 22 − 16 = 6 d = 6 22 + 6 = 28; 28 + 6 = 34

Printed Example 2(i), p 54.

Example 2(ii): a negative difference

Subtracting a negative changes the sign. All differences are −2, so keep adding −2, not +2.

1, −1, −3, −5, … −1 − 1 = −2 −3 − (−1) = −2 −5 − (−3) = −2 d = −2 −5 + (−2) = −7 −7 + (−2) = −9

Printed Example 2(ii), p 54.

Example 2(iii): alternating terms

The first two differences are unequal. This disproves the AP claim; there is no single common difference.

−2, 2, −2, 2, −2, … 2 − (−2) = 4 −2 − 2 = −4 4 ≠ −4

Printed Example 2(iii), p 54.

Example 2(iv): repeated blocks

The first two differences are zero, but the next is 1. Do not stop after the first matching differences. The list is not an AP.

1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 3, 3, … 1 − 1 = 0 1 − 1 = 0 2 − 1 = 1 0 ≠ 1

Printed Example 2(iv), p 55.

Our explanations and translations follow printed pp51-55. Worked questions and conclusions are from Examples1-2; the extra check in Example1 is ours.

Exercise 5.1 Q1(i-iv): model the situation

Decide which quantity is listed before comparing differences. Q1 completes the exercise coverage here, not the whole chapter.

5.1 Q1(i): Taxi fare: AP

First kilometre ka fare ₹15 hai aur har additional kilometre ka ₹8. 1, 2, 3 aur 4 km ke baad total fares compare karo, alag kilometre charges nahin. Har extra kilometre se total ₹8 badhta hai, isliye totals AP hain: a = 15 aur d = 8.

1 km: ₹15 2 km: ₹(15 + 8) = ₹23 3 km: ₹(15 + 2 × 8) = ₹31 4 km: ₹(15 + 3 × 8) = ₹39 23 − 15 = 8 31 − 23 = 8 39 − 31 = 8

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(i). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q1(ii): Bachi hui air: AP nahin

Shuru ki air A > 0 maano. Pump har baar us waqt bachi air ka one-fourth nikaalta hai, original amount ka one-fourth nahin. Isliye har operation ke baad previous amount ka three-fourths bachta hai. Loss chhota hota jaata hai, toh differences fixed nahin hain. Agar shuru mein A = 0 ho, toh all-zero list constant AP ka exception hai.

Operations 1, 2, 3 ke baad: 3A/4, 9A/16, 27A/64, … 9A/16 − 3A/4 = −3A/16 27A/64 − 9A/16 = −9A/64 −3A/16 ≠ −9A/64 (A > 0)

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(ii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q1(iii): Well: kis cost ki list?

First metre ka charge ₹150 hai; har next metre ka charge previous metre se ₹50 zyada hai. Har individual metre ke charges 150, 200, 250, 300, … hain: AP with d = 50. Isko running total mat bolo. Agar “har metre ke baad cost” ka matlab 1, 2, 3, 4 m depth tak cumulative bill ho, toh totals 150, 350, 600, 900, … hain aur AP nahin hain. Wording ka yeh distinction hamari vyakhya hai.

Har metre ka charge: ₹150, ₹200, ₹250, ₹300, … 200 − 150 = 50 250 − 200 = 50 300 − 250 = 50 Ab tak ka total: ₹150 ₹(150 + 200) = ₹350 ₹(150 + 200 + 250) = ₹600 ₹(150 + 200 + 250 + 300) = ₹900 350 − 150 = 200 600 − 350 = 250 900 − 600 = 300 200 ≠ 250

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q1(iv): Compound balance: AP nahin

Ek baar deposit kiye ₹10,000 par 8% yearly compound interest milta hai. Har saal naye balance par interest lagta hai, toh amount ko 1.08 se multiply karte hain. Yearly increase ₹800 se ₹864 aur phir ₹933.12 ho jaata hai; balances AP nahin hain. Yeh simple interest nahin hai, aur har saal naya ₹10,000 deposit bhi nahin.

Shuru mein: ₹10000 Year 1 ke baad: ₹10000 × 1.08 = ₹10800 Year 2 ke baad: ₹10800 × 1.08 = ₹11664 Year 3 ke baad: ₹11664 × 1.08 = ₹12597.12 10800 − 10000 = 800 11664 − 10800 = 864 12597.12 − 11664 = 933.12 800 ≠ 864

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iv). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q1(i): टैक्सी का किराया: AP

पहले किलोमीटर का किराया ₹15 है और हर अतिरिक्त किलोमीटर का ₹8 है। 1, 2, 3 और 4 km के बाद कुल किराए की तुलना करें, अलग-अलग किलोमीटर के शुल्क की नहीं। हर अतिरिक्त किलोमीटर से कुल किराया ₹8 बढ़ता है, इसलिए कुल किराए AP बनाते हैं: a = 15 और d = 8।

1 km: ₹15 2 km: ₹(15 + 8) = ₹23 3 km: ₹(15 + 2 × 8) = ₹31 4 km: ₹(15 + 3 × 8) = ₹39 23 − 15 = 8 31 − 23 = 8 39 − 31 = 8

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(i). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q1(ii): बची हुई हवा: AP नहीं

शुरुआती हवा A > 0 मानें। पंप हर बार उस समय बची हवा का एक चौथाई निकालता है, शुरुआती मात्रा का एक चौथाई नहीं। इसलिए हर क्रिया के बाद बची मात्रा पिछली मात्रा की तीन चौथाई है। निकली हवा की मात्रा घटती जाती है, इसलिए अंतर स्थिर नहीं हैं। यदि शुरू में A = 0 हो, तो सभी पद शून्य हैं; यह अपवाद स्थिर AP है।

क्रिया 1, 2, 3 के बाद: 3A/4, 9A/16, 27A/64, … 9A/16 − 3A/4 = −3A/16 27A/64 − 9A/16 = −9A/64 −3A/16 ≠ −9A/64 (A > 0)

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(ii). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q1(iii): कुआँ: किस लागत की सूची?

पहले मीटर की लागत ₹150 है; हर अगले मीटर का शुल्क पिछले मीटर से ₹50 अधिक है। प्रत्येक अलग मीटर के शुल्क की सूची 150, 200, 250, 300, … है: यह d = 50 वाली AP है। यह अब तक की कुल लागत नहीं है। यदि “हर मीटर के बाद लागत” से 1, 2, 3, 4 m गहराई तक की कुल लागत समझें, तो सूची 150, 350, 600, 900, … है और AP नहीं है। शब्दों का यह भेद हमारी व्याख्या है।

हर मीटर का शुल्क: ₹150, ₹200, ₹250, ₹300, … 200 − 150 = 50 250 − 200 = 50 300 − 250 = 50 अब तक की कुल लागत: ₹150 ₹(150 + 200) = ₹350 ₹(150 + 200 + 250) = ₹600 ₹(150 + 200 + 250 + 300) = ₹900 350 − 150 = 200 600 − 350 = 250 900 − 600 = 300 200 ≠ 250

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iii). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q1(iv): चक्रवृद्धि राशि: AP नहीं

एक बार जमा किए गए ₹10,000 पर सालाना 8% चक्रवृद्धि ब्याज मिलता है। हर साल नई राशि पर ब्याज लगता है, इसलिए राशि को 1.08 से गुणा करते हैं। सालाना वृद्धि ₹800 से ₹864 और फिर ₹933.12 हो जाती है; इसलिए राशियाँ AP नहीं हैं। यह साधारण ब्याज नहीं है और हर साल नया ₹10,000 जमा करना भी नहीं है।

शुरुआत: ₹10000 वर्ष 1 के बाद: ₹10000 × 1.08 = ₹10800 वर्ष 2 के बाद: ₹10800 × 1.08 = ₹11664 वर्ष 3 के बाद: ₹11664 × 1.08 = ₹12597.12 10800 − 10000 = 800 11664 − 10800 = 864 12597.12 − 11664 = 933.12 800 ≠ 864

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iv). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q1(i): Taxi fare: AP

The fare is ₹15 for the first kilometre and ₹8 for every additional kilometre. Compare total fares after 1, 2, 3 and 4 km, not just the separate kilometre charges. Every extra kilometre raises the total fare by ₹8, so the totals form an AP with a = 15 and d = 8.

1 km: ₹15 2 km: ₹(15 + 8) = ₹23 3 km: ₹(15 + 2 × 8) = ₹31 4 km: ₹(15 + 3 × 8) = ₹39 23 − 15 = 8 31 − 23 = 8 39 − 31 = 8

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(i). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

5.1 Q1(ii): Air remaining: not an AP

Let the initial amount of air be A > 0. The pump removes one quarter of the air currently remaining, not one quarter of the original amount each time. Therefore three quarters remains after each operation. The losses shrink, so the differences are not constant. If the cylinder starts empty (A = 0), the all-zero list is the degenerate AP exception.

After operations 1, 2, 3: 3A/4, 9A/16, 27A/64, … 9A/16 − 3A/4 = −3A/16 27A/64 − 9A/16 = −9A/64 −3A/16 ≠ −9A/64 (A > 0)

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(ii). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

5.1 Q1(iii): Well: name the quantity

The first metre costs ₹150; each subsequent metre costs ₹50 more than the previous metre. For the charge for each individual metre, the list is 150, 200, 250, 300, …: an AP with d = 50. Do not call that the running total. If “cost after every metre” means the cumulative bill after depths 1, 2, 3, 4 m, the totals are 150, 350, 600, 900, … and are not an AP. This distinction is our explanation of the wording.

Each metre: ₹150, ₹200, ₹250, ₹300, … 200 − 150 = 50 250 − 200 = 50 300 − 250 = 50 Cumulative totals: ₹150 ₹(150 + 200) = ₹350 ₹(150 + 200 + 250) = ₹600 ₹(150 + 200 + 250 + 300) = ₹900 350 − 150 = 200 600 − 350 = 250 900 − 600 = 300 200 ≠ 250

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iii). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

5.1 Q1(iv): Compound balance: not an AP

A single deposit of ₹10,000 earns compound interest at 8% per year. The interest is calculated on the new balance each year, so each balance is multiplied by 1.08. The annual increases grow from ₹800 to ₹864 to ₹933.12, so the balances are not an AP. This is not simple interest, and it is not a fresh ₹10,000 deposit every year.

Initial: ₹10000 After year 1: ₹10000 × 1.08 = ₹10800 After year 2: ₹10800 × 1.08 = ₹11664 After year 3: ₹11664 × 1.08 = ₹12597.12 10800 − 10000 = 800 11664 − 10800 = 864 12597.12 − 11664 = 933.12 800 ≠ 864

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q1(iv). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

Exercise 5.1 Q4(i-viii): AP or not?

Compare successive differences. For an AP, find d and write the next three terms. Q4(ix-xv) follows in the next section.

5.1 Q4(i): AP nahin hai

Double hone ka pattern hai, lekin same number add nahin hota. Pehle do differences unequal hain, isliye AP nahin hai.

2, 4, 8, 16, … 4 − 2 = 2 8 − 4 = 4 2 ≠ 4

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(i). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(ii): AP hai

Saare differences 1/2 hain. Diye pattern mein list AP hai; aage 1/2 teen baar add karo.

2, 5/2, 3, 7/2, … 5/2 − 2 = 1/2 3 − 5/2 = 1/2 7/2 − 3 = 1/2 d = 1/2 Agle teen terms: 4, 9/2, 5

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(ii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(iii): AP hai

Har step par −2 add hota hai. Yeh AP hai; next terms mein negative sign mat bhoolo.

−1.2, −3.2, −5.2, −7.2, … −3.2 − (−1.2) = −2 −5.2 − (−3.2) = −2 −7.2 − (−5.2) = −2 d = −2 Agle teen terms: −9.2, −11.2, −13.2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(iii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(iv): AP hai

Terms zero cross karte hain, lekin difference 4 hi hai. Yeh AP hai; har baar 4 add karo.

−10, −6, −2, 2, … −6 − (−10) = 4 −2 − (−6) = 4 2 − (−2) = 4 d = 4 Agle teen terms: 6, 10, 14

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(iv). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(v): AP hai

Subtract karne par 3 cancel hota hai aur har difference √2 hai. Irrational terms bhi AP bana sakte hain. Next term ke liye √2 add karo.

3, 3 + √2, 3 + 2√2, 3 + 3√2, … (3 + √2) − 3 = √2 (3 + 2√2) − (3 + √2) = √2 (3 + 3√2) − (3 + 2√2) = √2 d = √2 Agle teen terms: 3 + 4√2 3 + 5√2 3 + 6√2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(v). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(vi): AP nahin hai

Har baar ek extra digit 2 lagta hai, lekin numerical differences badalte hain. Yeh AP nahin hai.

0.2, 0.22, 0.222, 0.2222, … 0.22 − 0.2 = 0.02 0.222 − 0.22 = 0.002 0.2222 − 0.222 = 0.0002 0.02 ≠ 0.002

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(vi). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(vii): AP hai

Har term previous se 4 kam hai. Yeh AP hai aur d = −4 hai, +4 nahin.

0, −4, −8, −12, … −4 − 0 = −4 −8 − (−4) = −4 −12 − (−8) = −4 d = −4 Agle teen terms: −16, −20, −24

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(vii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(viii): AP hai

Equal terms bhi AP hain; common difference zero hai. Agle teen terms bhi −1/2 rahenge.

−1/2, −1/2, −1/2, −1/2, … −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 d = 0 Agle teen terms: −1/2, −1/2, −1/2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(viii). Hamari vyakhya aur translation; official answer key nahin.

5.1 Q4(i): AP नहीं है

दोगुना करने का पैटर्न है, लेकिन एक ही संख्या नहीं जुड़ती। पहले दो अंतर असमान हैं, इसलिए यह AP नहीं है।

2, 4, 8, 16, … 4 − 2 = 2 8 − 4 = 4 2 ≠ 4

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(i). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q4(ii): AP है

सभी अंतर 1/2 हैं। बताए गए पैटर्न में यह AP है; आगे 1/2 तीन बार जोड़ें।

2, 5/2, 3, 7/2, … 5/2 − 2 = 1/2 3 − 5/2 = 1/2 7/2 − 3 = 1/2 d = 1/2 अगले तीन पद: 4, 9/2, 5

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(ii). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q4(iii): AP है

हर चरण में −2 जुड़ता है। यह AP है; अगले पद निकालते समय ऋण चिह्न बनाए रखें।

−1.2, −3.2, −5.2, −7.2, … −3.2 − (−1.2) = −2 −5.2 − (−3.2) = −2 −7.2 − (−5.2) = −2 d = −2 अगले तीन पद: −9.2, −11.2, −13.2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(iii). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q4(iv): AP है

पद शून्य के पार जाते हैं, पर अंतर 4 ही रहता है। यह AP है; हर बार 4 जोड़ें।

−10, −6, −2, 2, … −6 − (−10) = 4 −2 − (−6) = 4 2 − (−2) = 4 d = 4 अगले तीन पद: 6, 10, 14

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(iv). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q4(v): AP है

घटाने पर 3 कट जाता है और हर अंतर √2 है। अपरिमेय पद भी AP बना सकते हैं। हर अगले पद के लिए √2 जोड़ें।

3, 3 + √2, 3 + 2√2, 3 + 3√2, … (3 + √2) − 3 = √2 (3 + 2√2) − (3 + √2) = √2 (3 + 3√2) − (3 + 2√2) = √2 d = √2 अगले तीन पद: 3 + 4√2 3 + 5√2 3 + 6√2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(v). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

5.1 Q4(vi): AP नहीं है

हर बार एक नया अंक 2 जुड़ता दिखता है, लेकिन संख्यात्मक अंतर बदलते हैं। यह AP नहीं है।

0.2, 0.22, 0.222, 0.2222, … 0.22 − 0.2 = 0.02 0.222 − 0.22 = 0.002 0.2222 − 0.222 = 0.0002 0.02 ≠ 0.002

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p55, Exercise 5.1 Q4(vi). हमारी व्याख्या और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

हर पद पिछले पद से 4 कम है। यह AP है और d = −4 है, +4 नहीं।

0, −4, −8, −12, … −4 − 0 = −4 −8 − (−4) = −4 −12 − (−8) = −4 d = −4 अगले तीन पद: −16, −20, −24

−1/2, −1/2, −1/2, −1/2, … −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 d = 0 अगले तीन पद: −1/2, −1/2, −1/2

5.1 Q4(i): Not an AP

Doubling gives a pattern, but not a fixed addition. The first two differences are unequal, so this is not an AP.

2, 4, 8, 16, … 4 − 2 = 2 8 − 4 = 4 2 ≠ 4

5.1 Q4(ii): AP

The differences are all 1/2. The list is an AP under the indicated pattern; add 1/2 three more times.

2, 5/2, 3, 7/2, … 5/2 − 2 = 1/2 3 − 5/2 = 1/2 7/2 − 3 = 1/2 d = 1/2 Next three terms: 4, 9/2, 5

5.1 Q4(iii): AP

Each step adds −2. This is an AP; keep the negative sign when extending it.

−1.2, −3.2, −5.2, −7.2, … −3.2 − (−1.2) = −2 −5.2 − (−3.2) = −2 −7.2 − (−5.2) = −2 d = −2 Next three terms: −9.2, −11.2, −13.2

5.1 Q4(iv): AP

The terms cross zero, but the difference stays 4. The list is an AP; add 4 each time.

−10, −6, −2, 2, … −6 − (−10) = 4 −2 − (−6) = 4 2 − (−2) = 4 d = 4 Next three terms: 6, 10, 14

5.1 Q4(v): AP

The 3 cancels in subtraction and each difference is √2. Irrational terms can form an AP too. Add √2 for each next term.

3, 3 + √2, 3 + 2√2, 3 + 3√2, … (3 + √2) − 3 = √2 (3 + 2√2) − (3 + √2) = √2 (3 + 3√2) − (3 + 2√2) = √2 d = √2 Next three terms: 3 + 4√2 3 + 5√2 3 + 6√2

5.1 Q4(vi): Not an AP

An extra digit 2 is appended each time, but the numerical differences change. This is not an AP.

0.2, 0.22, 0.222, 0.2222, … 0.22 − 0.2 = 0.02 0.222 − 0.22 = 0.002 0.2222 − 0.222 = 0.0002 0.02 ≠ 0.002

5.1 Q4(vii): AP

Each term is 4 less than the previous term. This is an AP with d = −4, not +4.

0, −4, −8, −12, … −4 − 0 = −4 −8 − (−4) = −4 −12 − (−8) = −4 d = −4 Next three terms: −16, −20, −24

Equal terms form an AP with common difference zero. The next three terms remain −1/2.

−1/2, −1/2, −1/2, −1/2, … −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 −1/2 − (−1/2) = 0 d = 0 Next three terms: −1/2, −1/2, −1/2

5.1 Q4(x): AP

5.1 Q4(xi): Depends on a

Not an AP for a general real a. Our parameter check adds two exceptions: a = 0 or a = 1 gives a constant AP with d = 0. Any other real value makes the first two differences unequal. Checking these values also confirms all later terms stay constant.

a, a², a³, a⁴, … First two differences: a² − a = a(a − 1) a³ − a² = a²(a − 1) Equal only if: a²(a − 1) − a(a − 1) = 0 a(a − 1)² = 0 a = 0 or a = 1 a = 0: 0, 0, 0, 0, …; d = 0 Next: 0, 0, 0 a = 1: 1, 1, 1, 1, …; d = 0 Next: 1, 1, 1

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p56, Exercise 5.1 Q4(xi). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

5.1 Q4(xii): AP

√2, √8, √18, √32, … √8 = 2√2 √18 = 3√2 √32 = 4√2 2√2 − √2 = √2 3√2 − 2√2 = √2 4√2 − 3√2 = √2 d = √2 Next three terms: 5√2 6√2 7√2

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p56, Exercise 5.1 Q4(xii). Our explanations and translations, not an official answer key.

5.1 Q4(xiii): Not an AP

The first two differences are unequal. Our exact check assumes they are equal and reaches the false statement √3 = 2. Therefore this is not an AP; no rounded decimals are needed.

√3, √6, √9, √12, … d₁ = √6 − √3 d₂ = 3 − √6 If d₁ = d₂: 2√6 = 3 + √3 24 = 12 + 6√3 √3 = 2 (false) So d₁ ≠ d₂

nth term: formula, salary and Examples 3-4

Count equal steps, then check the term position. Translations, extra checks and revision below are ours.

nth term: n − 1 kyun?

First term a hai. Term 2 tak d ek baar aur term 3 tak d do baar add hota hai. Term 1 se term n tak n − 1 equal steps hain, isliye aₙ = a + (n − 1)d. Index n positive integer hai. Finite AP mein n total terms se zyada nahin ho sakta. Last term ko l = aₙ bhi likhte hain. Formula ek term deta hai, terms ka sum nahin.

a₁ = a = a + 0d a₂ = a + d a₃ = a + 2d a₄ = a + 3d aₙ = a + (n − 1)d n = 1 check: a₁ = a + 0d = a

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Reena: year n ki monthly salary

Source mein Reena ki starting monthly salary ₹8,000 hai aur annual increment ₹500 hai. Har year ki monthly salary list karo, un years ki total earnings nahin. Fifth year tak four increments hote hain. Same rule continue ho toh 15th aur 25th years tak 14 aur 24 increments honge.

a = ₹8000; d = ₹500 a₅ = 8000 + (5 − 1) × 500 = ₹10000 har month a₁₅ = 8000 + 14 × 500 = ₹15000 har month a₂₅ = 8000 + 24 × 500 = ₹20000 har month

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp56-57. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Printed Example 3: 10th term

2, 7, 12, … mein pehle a = 2 aur d = 7 − 2 = 5 pehchaano. Tenth term first se nine steps aage hai, ten steps nahin. Values put karne par 47 milta hai.

a = 2; d = 5; n = 10 a₁₀ = a + (10 − 1)d = 2 + 9 × 5 = 2 + 45 = 47 Check: a₉ = 42; a₁₀ − a₉ = 5

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Printed Example 4: term ka index

21, 18, 15, … ka common difference −3 hai. Yahan term value −81 di hai aur index n unknown hai. n solve karke positive integer hona check karo. 35th term −81 hai. Alag equation se eighth term zero milta hai. Term value negative ya zero ho sakti hai, lekin positions 1 se start hoti hain.

a = 21; d = −3 −81 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) −81 = 24 − 3n −105 = −3n n = 35 Check: 21 + 34(−3) = −81 Zero term ke liye: 0 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) 3(n − 1) = 21 n = 8 Check: 21 + 7(−3) = 0

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Quick revision: value aur index

Hamari revision checklist: pehle AP confirm karo, d ka sign rakho aur n − 1 additions count karo. d ≠ 0 par target value ke liye n = 1 + (target − a)/d solve karo. n positive integer aur finite range ke andar ho tabhi target term hai. d = 0 par har available term a hai; division test valid nahin. Term negative ho sakta hai, lekin index negative ya fractional nahin.

aₙ = a + (n − 1)d d ≠ 0: n = 1 + (target − a)/d n = 1, 2, 3, … d = 0: aₙ = a N terms wali finite AP: 1 ≤ n ≤ N

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57-58. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

nवाँ पद: n − 1 क्यों?

पहला पद a है। दूसरे पद तक पहुँचने के लिए d एक बार और तीसरे तक d दो बार जोड़ते हैं। पहले से nवें पद तक n − 1 समान चरण हैं, इसलिए aₙ = a + (n − 1)d। स्थान n एक धनात्मक पूर्णांक है। परिमित AP में n पदों की कुल संख्या से अधिक नहीं होना चाहिए। अंतिम पद को l = aₙ भी लिखते हैं। सूत्र एक पद देता है, पदों का योग नहीं।

a₁ = a = a + 0d a₂ = a + d a₃ = a + 2d a₄ = a + 3d aₙ = a + (n − 1)d n = 1 की जाँच: a₁ = a + 0d = a

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

रीना: nवें वर्ष का मासिक वेतन

स्रोत में रीना का शुरुआती मासिक वेतन ₹8,000 है और सालाना वृद्धि ₹500 है। हर वर्ष लागू मासिक वेतन की सूची बनाएँ, उन वर्षों की कुल कमाई की नहीं। पाँचवें वर्ष तक चार बढ़ोतरी होती हैं। यही नियम जारी रहे, तो 15वें और 25वें वर्ष तक क्रमशः 14 और 24 बढ़ोतरी होंगी।

a = ₹8000; d = ₹500 a₅ = 8000 + (5 − 1) × 500 = ₹10000 प्रति माह a₁₅ = 8000 + 14 × 500 = ₹15000 प्रति माह a₂₅ = 8000 + 24 × 500 = ₹20000 प्रति माह

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp56-57. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

मुद्रित उदाहरण 3: 10वाँ पद

2, 7, 12, … में पहले a = 2 और d = 7 − 2 = 5 पहचानें। दसवाँ पद पहले पद से नौ चरण आगे है, दस चरण नहीं। सूत्र में मान रखने पर 47 मिलता है।

a = 2; d = 5; n = 10 a₁₀ = a + (10 − 1)d = 2 + 9 × 5 = 2 + 45 = 47 जाँच: a₉ = 42; a₁₀ − a₉ = 5

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

मुद्रित उदाहरण 4: पद का स्थान

21, 18, 15, … का सार्व अंतर −3 है। यहाँ पद का मान −81 दिया है और स्थान n अज्ञात है। n निकालकर जाँचें कि वह धनात्मक पूर्णांक है। 35वाँ पद −81 है। अलग समीकरण से आठवाँ पद शून्य मिलता है। पद का मान ऋणात्मक या शून्य हो सकता है, पर स्थान की गिनती 1 से शुरू होती है।

a = 21; d = −3 −81 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) −81 = 24 − 3n −105 = −3n n = 35 जाँच: 21 + 34(−3) = −81 शून्य पद के लिए: 0 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) 3(n − 1) = 21 n = 8 जाँच: 21 + 7(−3) = 0

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

त्वरित दोहराव: पद का मान और स्थान

हमारी दोहराव सूची: पहले AP की पुष्टि करें, d का चिह्न सही रखें और n − 1 बार जोड़ना गिनें। d ≠ 0 पर किसी लक्ष्य मान का स्थान निकालने के लिए n = 1 + (लक्ष्य − a)/d हल करें। n धनात्मक पूर्णांक और किसी भी परिमित सीमा के भीतर हो, तभी लक्ष्य पद है। d = 0 पर हर उपलब्ध पद a है; भाग देने वाला परीक्षण लागू नहीं है। पद ऋणात्मक हो सकता है, पर ऋणात्मक या भिन्नात्मक स्थान नहीं होता।

aₙ = a + (n − 1)d d ≠ 0: n = 1 + (लक्ष्य − a)/d n = 1, 2, 3, … d = 0: aₙ = a N पदों वाली परिमित AP: 1 ≤ n ≤ N

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57-58. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

The nth term: why n − 1?

The first term is a. Reaching term 2 adds d once; reaching term 3 adds d twice. From term 1 to term n there are n − 1 equal steps, so aₙ = a + (n − 1)d. The index n is a positive integer. A finite AP also requires n not to exceed the number of its terms. Its last term may be written l = aₙ. The formula gives one term, not the sum of terms.

a₁ = a = a + 0d a₂ = a + d a₃ = a + 2d a₄ = a + 3d aₙ = a + (n − 1)d Check n = 1: a₁ = a + 0d = a

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Reena: monthly salary in year n

The source starts Reena at a monthly salary of ₹8,000, with a ₹500 annual increment. List the monthly salary applicable in each year, not the total earned over those years. The fifth year has four increments. Assuming the same rule continues, the 15th and 25th years have 14 and 24 increments.

a = ₹8000; d = ₹500 a₅ = 8000 + (5 − 1) × 500 = ₹10000 per month a₁₅ = 8000 + 14 × 500 = ₹15000 per month a₂₅ = 8000 + 24 × 500 = ₹20000 per month

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp56-57. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Printed Example 3: find the 10th term

For 2, 7, 12, …, first identify a = 2 and d = 7 − 2 = 5. The tenth term is nine steps after the first, not ten steps. Substitution gives 47.

a = 2; d = 5; n = 10 a₁₀ = a + (10 − 1)d = 2 + 9 × 5 = 2 + 45 = 47 Check: a₉ = 42; a₁₀ − a₉ = 5

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Printed Example 4: find the index

For 21, 18, 15, … the common difference is −3. Here the term value −81 is known and the index n is unknown. Solve for n, then check it is a positive integer. The 35th term is −81. A separate equation shows the eighth term is zero. Negative or zero term values are allowed; their positions still start at 1.

a = 21; d = −3 −81 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) −81 = 24 − 3n −105 = −3n n = 35 Check: 21 + 34(−3) = −81 For a zero term: 0 = 21 + (n − 1)(−3) 3(n − 1) = 21 n = 8 Check: 21 + 7(−3) = 0

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp58. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Quick revision: value versus index

Our revision checklist: confirm the list is an AP, keep the sign of d, and count n − 1 additions. To test whether a target value occurs when d ≠ 0, solve n = 1 + (target − a)/d. Accept it only if n is a positive integer and lies within any finite range. If d = 0, every available term equals a; the division test is invalid. A negative value can be a term, but a negative or fractional index cannot be a position.

aₙ = a + (n − 1)d d ≠ 0: n = 1 + (target − a)/d n = 1, 2, 3, … d = 0: aₙ = a Finite AP with N terms: 1 ≤ n ≤ N

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp57-58. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Printed Examples 5-7: recover, test and count

Build the AP from known terms, reject a fractional index, and count both endpoints.

Printed Example 5: AP kaise nikalein?

Third term 5 aur seventh term 9 hai. Dono positions ke beech four steps hain, isliye term equations subtract karke d nikalo. Phir kisi ek equation mein value rakhkar first term a nikalo. Required AP hai: 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … .

a₃ = a + 2d = 5 a₇ = a + 6d = 9 (a + 6d) − (a + 2d) = 9 − 5 4d = 4 ⇒ d = 1 a + 2 = 5 ⇒ a = 3 AP: 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … Check: a₃ = 3 + 2 × 1 = 5 a₇ = 3 + 6 × 1 = 9

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p58, Example 5. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Printed Example 6: kya 301 term hai?

AP 5, 11, 17, 23, … ka common difference 6 hai. nth term ko 301 ke equal rakhkar position solve karo. Result 151/3 integer nahin hai, isliye 301 is AP ka term nahin hai. Fractional position ko round karke valid index mat banao. Hamari neighbour check mein 301, 50th aur 51st terms ke beech hai.

11 − 5 = 17 − 11 = 23 − 17 = 6 a = 5; d = 6 301 = 5 + (n − 1)6 301 = 6n − 1 302 = 6n n = 302/6 = 151/3 151/3 ∉ {1, 2, 3, …} 301 is AP ka term nahin hai Check: a₅₀ = 5 + 49 × 6 = 299 a₅₁ = 5 + 50 × 6 = 305

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p59, Example 6. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Printed Example 7: two-digit multiples of 3 gino

3 ka first two-digit multiple 12 aur last 99 hai. List 12, 15, 18, …, 99 ek AP hai jiska difference 3 hai. Total terms ginne ke liye 99 ki position nikalo. Gaps 29 hain, par numbers 30, kyunki dono endpoints included hain. Gap-count explanation aur endpoint checks hamare hain.

a = 12; d = 3; aₙ = 99 99 = 12 + (n − 1)3 87 = 3(n − 1) n − 1 = 29 n = 30 Check: 12 = 3 × 4; 99 = 3 × 33 33 − 4 + 1 = 30 9 one-digit hai; 102 three-digit hai

मुद्रित उदाहरण 5: AP ज्ञात करें

तीसरा पद 5 और सातवाँ पद 9 है। इन स्थानों के बीच चार चरण हैं, इसलिए दोनों पदों के समीकरण घटाकर d निकालें। फिर किसी एक समीकरण में मान रखकर पहला पद a निकालें। वांछित AP है: 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … ।

a₃ = a + 2d = 5 a₇ = a + 6d = 9 (a + 6d) − (a + 2d) = 9 − 5 4d = 4 ⇒ d = 1 a + 2 = 5 ⇒ a = 3 AP: 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … जाँच: a₃ = 3 + 2 × 1 = 5 a₇ = 3 + 6 × 1 = 9

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p58, Example 5. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

मुद्रित उदाहरण 6: क्या 301 एक पद है?

AP 5, 11, 17, 23, … का सार्व अंतर 6 है। nवें पद को 301 के बराबर रखकर उसका स्थान निकालें। परिणाम 151/3 पूर्णांक नहीं है, इसलिए 301 इस AP का पद नहीं है। भिन्नात्मक स्थान को निकटतम पूर्णांक में बदलना सही नहीं। हमारी पड़ोसी पदों की जाँच में 301, 50वें और 51वें पद के बीच आता है।

11 − 5 = 17 − 11 = 23 − 17 = 6 a = 5; d = 6 301 = 5 + (n − 1)6 301 = 6n − 1 302 = 6n n = 302/6 = 151/3 151/3 ∉ {1, 2, 3, …} 301 इस AP का पद नहीं है जाँच: a₅₀ = 5 + 49 × 6 = 299 a₅₁ = 5 + 50 × 6 = 305

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p59, Example 6. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

मुद्रित उदाहरण 7: दो अंकों के 3 के गुणज

3 से विभाज्य सबसे छोटी दो अंकों की संख्या 12 और सबसे बड़ी 99 है। सूची 12, 15, 18, …, 99 में सार्व अंतर 3 है। सभी पद गिनने के लिए 99 का स्थान निकालें। अंतराल 29 हैं, पर संख्याएँ 30 हैं क्योंकि दोनों छोर शामिल हैं। अंतराल गिनने की व्याख्या और छोरों की जाँच हमारी है।

a = 12; d = 3; aₙ = 99 99 = 12 + (n − 1)3 87 = 3(n − 1) n − 1 = 29 n = 30 जाँच: 12 = 3 × 4; 99 = 3 × 33 33 − 4 + 1 = 30 9 एक अंक का है; 102 तीन अंकों का है

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p59, Example 7. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

Printed Example 5: recover the AP

The third term is 5 and the seventh term is 9. These positions are four steps apart, so subtract the two term equations to find d. Then substitute back to find the first term a. The requested AP is 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … .

a₃ = a + 2d = 5 a₇ = a + 6d = 9 (a + 6d) − (a + 2d) = 9 − 5 4d = 4 ⇒ d = 1 a + 2 = 5 ⇒ a = 3 AP: 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, … Check: a₃ = 3 + 2 × 1 = 5 a₇ = 3 + 6 × 1 = 9

Printed Example 6: is 301 a term?

For the AP 5, 11, 17, 23, …, the common difference is 6. Set the nth term equal to 301 and solve for its position. The result 151/3 is not an integer, so 301 is not a term. Do not round a fractional position into a valid index. Our neighbouring-term check confirms that 301 lies between the 50th and 51st terms.

11 − 5 = 17 − 11 = 23 − 17 = 6 a = 5; d = 6 301 = 5 + (n − 1)6 301 = 6n − 1 302 = 6n n = 302/6 = 151/3 151/3 ∉ {1, 2, 3, …} 301 is not a term Check: a₅₀ = 5 + 49 × 6 = 299 a₅₁ = 5 + 50 × 6 = 305

Printed Example 7: count two-digit multiples of 3

The first two-digit multiple of 3 is 12 and the last is 99. Their list 12, 15, 18, …, 99 is an AP with difference 3. Solve for the position of 99 to count all terms. There are 29 gaps but 30 numbers, because both endpoints are included. The gap-count explanation and endpoint checks are ours.

a = 12; d = 3; aₙ = 99 99 = 12 + (n − 1)3 87 = 3(n − 1) n − 1 = 29 n = 30 Check: 12 = 3 × 4; 99 = 3 × 33 33 − 4 + 1 = 30 9 has one digit; 102 has three digits

Printed Examples 8-9: reverse counting and simple interest

Count endpoints correctly and distinguish accumulated interest from the balance.

Printed Example 8: end se 11th term

Finite AP hai: 10, 7, 4, …, −62. Pehle total 25 terms nikalo. Last term ko position 1 gino, toh end se 11th term ki start se position 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 hai, 14 nahin. Dono printed methods se −32 milta hai. AP reverse karne par first term −62 aur difference +3 hoga; difference −3 nahin rahega.

a = 10; d = −3; last term = −62 −62 = 10 + (N − 1)(−3) −72 = −3(N − 1) N − 1 = 24 ⇒ N = 25 Start se position: 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 a₁₅ = 10 + 14(−3) = −32 Reverse AP: −62, −59, −56, …, 10 Reverse a = −62; reverse d = +3 Reverse a₁₁ = −62 + 10 × 3 = −32

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp59-60, Example 8. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Printed Example 9: 30 years ka simple interest

₹1,000 par 8% yearly simple interest hai. 1st, 2nd, 3rd, … year ke end par interest ₹80, ₹160, ₹240, … hai (simple interest, isliye interest par interest nahin). Yeh amounts a = 80 aur d = 80 wali AP hain. 30th term ₹2,400 hai: 30 years ke baad ka interest, account balance nahin. Har alag year mein extra interest ₹80 hai. In yearly figures ko aapas mein add mat karo; har figure mein pichhle saalon ka interest pehle se shamil hai.

P = ₹1000; R = 8% per year I(t) = P × R × t / 100 = ₹80t I(1) = ₹80; I(2) = ₹160; I(3) = ₹240 a = ₹80; d = ₹80 a₃₀ = 80 + (30 − 1) × 80 = 80 + 2320 = ₹2400 interest Check: 1000 × 8 × 30 / 100 = ₹2400 Hamara distinction: principal + interest = ₹1000 + ₹2400 = ₹3400 balance

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp60-61, Example 9. Hamari vyakhya, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

मुद्रित उदाहरण 8: अंत से 11वाँ पद

परिमित AP है: 10, 7, 4, …, −62। पहले पदों की कुल संख्या 25 निकालें। अंतिम पद को स्थान 1 गिनने पर अंत से 11वें पद का आरंभ से स्थान 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 है, 14 नहीं। दोनों मुद्रित विधियों से −32 मिलता है। AP उलटने पर पहला पद −62 और सार्व अंतर +3 होगा; सार्व अंतर −3 नहीं रहेगा।

a = 10; d = −3; अंतिम पद = −62 −62 = 10 + (N − 1)(−3) −72 = −3(N − 1) N − 1 = 24 ⇒ N = 25 आरंभ से स्थान: 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 a₁₅ = 10 + 14(−3) = −32 उलटी AP: −62, −59, −56, …, 10 उलटा a = −62; उलटा d = +3 उलटा a₁₁ = −62 + 10 × 3 = −32

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp59-60, Example 8. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

मुद्रित उदाहरण 9: 30 वर्ष बाद साधारण ब्याज

₹1,000 पर 8% वार्षिक साधारण ब्याज है। 1ले, 2रे, 3रे, … वर्ष के अंत में ब्याज ₹80, ₹160, ₹240, … है (साधारण ब्याज, इसलिए ब्याज पर ब्याज नहीं)। ये राशियाँ a = 80 और d = 80 वाली AP हैं। इसका 30वाँ पद ₹2,400 है: यह 30 वर्ष बाद का ब्याज है, खाते की कुल राशि नहीं। प्रत्येक अलग वर्ष में अतिरिक्त ब्याज ₹80 है। इन वार्षिक राशियों को आपस में न जोड़ें; हर राशि में पिछले वर्षों का ब्याज पहले से शामिल है।

P = ₹1000; R = 8% प्रति वर्ष I(t) = P × R × t / 100 = ₹80t I(1) = ₹80; I(2) = ₹160; I(3) = ₹240 a = ₹80; d = ₹80 a₃₀ = 80 + (30 − 1) × 80 = 80 + 2320 = ₹2400 ब्याज जाँच: 1000 × 8 × 30 / 100 = ₹2400 हमारा स्पष्टीकरण: मूलधन + ब्याज = ₹1000 + ₹2400 = ₹3400 कुल राशि

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp60-61, Example 9. हमारी व्याख्या, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

Printed Example 8: 11th term from the end

The finite AP is 10, 7, 4, …, −62. First find its 25 terms. Counting the last term as position 1, the 11th term from the end is position 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 from the start, not position 14. Both printed methods give −32. Reversing the AP changes its first term to −62 and its difference to +3; it does not keep −3.

a = 10; d = −3; last term = −62 −62 = 10 + (N − 1)(−3) −72 = −3(N − 1) N − 1 = 24 ⇒ N = 25 From the start: 25 − 11 + 1 = 15 a₁₅ = 10 + 14(−3) = −32 Reverse AP: −62, −59, −56, …, 10 Reverse a = −62; reverse d = +3 Reverse a₁₁ = −62 + 10 × 3 = −32

Printed Example 9: simple interest after 30 years

₹1,000 is invested at 8% simple interest per year. The interest at the end of the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, … year is ₹80, ₹160, ₹240, … (simple interest, so no interest on interest). These amounts form an AP with a = 80 and d = 80. Its 30th term is ₹2,400: the interest after 30 years, not the account balance. The extra interest earned during each single year is ₹80. Do not add these yearly figures together; each already includes the earlier years' interest.

P = ₹1000; R = 8% per year I(t) = P × R × t / 100 = ₹80t I(1) = ₹80; I(2) = ₹160; I(3) = ₹240 a = ₹80; d = ₹80 a₃₀ = 80 + (30 − 1) × 80 = 80 + 2320 = ₹2400 interest Check: 1000 × 8 × 30 / 100 = ₹2400 Our distinction: principal + interest = ₹1000 + ₹2400 = ₹3400 balance

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, pp60-61, Example 9. Our explanations, added checks and translations, not an official answer key.

Example 10 and Exercise 5.2 Q1: count rows and fill the table

All five Q1 blanks, with substitutions and checks. Exercise 5.2 is not complete.

Printed Example 10: rose plants ki rows gino

Rows mein rose plants 23, 21, 19, …, 5 hain. Har next row mein 2 plants kam hain, isliye d = −2. Last row nth term hai. Answer 10 rows hai, 9 nahin: 10 rows ke beech 9 decreases hote hain. Sawaal rows ki count ka hai, total plants ka nahin.

a = 23; d = 21 − 23 = −2; aₙ = 5 5 = 23 + (n − 1)(−2) −18 = −2(n − 1) n − 1 = 9 ⇒ n = 10 23 + 9(−2) = 5 23, 21, 19, 17, 15, 13, 11, 9, 7, 5

aₙ = a + (n − 1)d a₈ = 7 + (8 − 1) × 3 = 7 + 21 = 28 7, 10, 13, 16, 19, 22, 25, 28

Printed Example 10: count the rose rows

The rows contain 23, 21, 19, …, 5 rose plants. Each next row has 2 fewer plants, so d = −2. The last row is the nth term. Solving gives 10 rows, not 9: there are 9 decreases between 10 rows. This asks for the number of rows, not the total number of plants.

a = 23; d = 21 − 23 = −2; aₙ = 5 5 = 23 + (n − 1)(−2) −18 = −2(n − 1) n − 1 = 9 ⇒ n = 10 23 + 9(−2) = 5 23, 21, 19, 17, 15, 13, 11, 9, 7, 5

0 = −18 + (10 − 1)d 18 = 9d ⇒ d = 2 −18 + 9 × 2 = 0

a₁₀₅ = 3.5 + (105 − 1) × 0 = 3.5 + 104 × 0 = 3.5 3.5, 3.5, 3.5, …

Exercise 5.2 Q3(i): One missing term between 2 and 26

The three terms are 2, [blank], 26. There are two equal gaps between the endpoints, so divide their difference by 2. Missing term: 14.

26 = 2 + 2d d = (26 − 2)/2 = 12 a₂ = 2 + 12 = 14 2, 14, 26 14 − 2 = 26 − 14 = 12

Exercise 5.2 Q3(ii): Find the first and third terms

The four positions are [blank], 13, [blank], 3. From the second to the fourth term there are two gaps. Hence d = −5. Missing terms, left to right: 18 and 8.

3 = 13 + 2d d = (3 − 13)/2 = −5 a₁ = 13 − (−5) = 18 a₃ = 13 + (−5) = 8 18, 13, 8, 3 13 − 18 = 8 − 13 = 3 − 8 = −5

Exercise 5.2 Q3(iii): Read the mixed number 9½ correctly

9½ = 19/2 d = (19/2 − 5)/3 = (9/2)/3 = 3/2 a₂ = 5 + 3/2 = 13/2 = 6½ a₃ = 5 + 2 × 3/2 = 8 5, 6½, 8, 9½ 6½ − 5 = 8 − 6½ = 9½ − 8 = 1½

The row has six terms: −4, four blanks, 6. Six terms mean five gaps. Thus d = 2. Missing terms, left to right: −2, 0, 2, 4.

6 = −4 + 5d d = (6 + 4)/5 = 2 −4, −2, 0, 2, 4, 6 −2 − (−4) = 0 − (−2) = 2 2 − 0 = 4 − 2 = 6 − 4 = 2

Exercise 5.2 Q4-Q6: indices, finite counts and membership

Four worked parts. Count the endpoints and reject a non-integer index without rounding.

Exercise 5.2 Q4: 78 kaunsa term hai?

a = 3; d = 8 − 3 = 5 78 = 3 + (n − 1) × 5 75 = 5(n − 1) n − 1 = 15 ⇒ n = 16 a₁₆ = 3 + 15 × 5 = 78

a = 7; d = 6; aₙ = 205 205 = 7 + (n − 1) × 6 198 = 6(n − 1) n − 1 = 33 ⇒ n = 34 a₃₄ = 7 + 33 × 6 = 205

Exercise 5.2 Q5(i): Count the terms up to 205

The finite AP is 7, 13, 19, …, 205. Its difference is 6. The endpoint calculation gives 33 gaps, hence 34 terms including both 7 and 205. The requested answer is a count, not the sum of the terms.

a = 7; d = 6; aₙ = 205 205 = 7 + (n − 1) × 6 198 = 6(n − 1) n − 1 = 33 ⇒ n = 34 a₃₄ = 7 + 33 × 6 = 205

Exercise 5.2 Q5(ii): Count a decreasing fractional AP

The finite AP is 18, 15½, 13, …, −47. Here 15½ = 31/2 and d = −5/2. There are 26 decreases of 2½, hence 27 terms. A negative difference does not make the number of terms negative.

15½ = 31/2 d = 31/2 − 18 = −5/2 13 − 31/2 = −5/2 −47 = 18 + (n − 1)(−5/2) −65 = (n − 1)(−5/2) n − 1 = (−65) × (−2/5) = 26 n = 27 a₂₇ = 18 + 26(−5/2) = −47

Exercise 5.2 Q6: Is −150 a term? Test the index

The AP is 11, 8, 5, 2, … with d = −3. Setting its nth term to −150 gives n = 164/3, which is not an integer. Therefore −150 is not a term. Do not round n: the neighbouring terms are −148 and −151, so the sequence skips −150.

−150 = 11 + (n − 1)(−3) −161 = −3(n − 1) n − 1 = 161/3 n = 164/3 = 54⅔ a₅₄ = 11 + 53(−3) = −148 a₅₅ = 11 + 54(−3) = −151 −151 < −150 < −148

Exercise 5.2 Q7-Q10: recover the AP from indexed terms

Four worked questions. Subtract the indices to count the gaps, then recover the required term or difference.

Exercise 5.2 Q7: Do indexed terms se d nikalo

11th term 38 aur 16th term 73 hai. Index gap 5 hai, isliye 5d = 35 aur d = 7. 11th se 31st term tak 20 gaps hain, toh answer 178 hai. Hamari extra check mein a = −32 nikaal kar dono given terms verify kiye hain.

a₁₆ − a₁₁ = (16 − 11)d 73 − 38 = 5d ⇒ d = 7 a₃₁ = a₁₁ + (31 − 11)d a₃₁ = 38 + 20 × 7 = 178 a = 38 − 10 × 7 = −32 a₁₁ = −32 + 70 = 38 a₁₆ = −32 + 105 = 73

Exercise 5.2 Q8: Last term ko 50th term lo

a₅₀ − a₃ = (50 − 3)d 106 − 12 = 47d 94 = 47d ⇒ d = 2 a₂₉ = a₃ + (29 − 3)d a₂₉ = 12 + 26 × 2 = 64 a = 12 − 2 × 2 = 8 a₅₀ = 8 + 49 × 2 = 106

a₁₇ = a₁₀ + 7 a₁₇ − a₁₀ = 7 (a + 16d) − (a + 9d) = 7 7d = 7 ⇒ d = 1 Check: (17 − 10) × 1 = 7

Exercise 5.2 Q9: Find the index of the zero term

The 3rd and 9th terms are 4 and −8. Across 6 gaps the value changes by −12, so d = −2. Then a = 8. Solving aₙ = 0 gives n = 5, so the 5th term is zero. Zero is the term value, not its position; indices start at 1.

a₉ − a₃ = (9 − 3)d −8 − 4 = 6d ⇒ d = −2 a = 4 − 2(−2) = 8 0 = 8 + (n − 1)(−2) 2(n − 1) = 8 ⇒ n = 5 a₅ = 8 + 4(−2) = 0 a₉ = 8 + 8(−2) = −8

Exercise 5.2 Q10: Translate "exceeds by 7" correctly

The 17th term exceeds the 10th term by 7, so a₁₇ − a₁₀ = 7. The first term cancels, leaving 7d = 7 and d = 1. The question determines d, not a unique first term. Our check: any AP with d = 1 rises by 7 across these 7 gaps.

a₁₇ = a₁₀ + 7 a₁₇ − a₁₀ = 7 (a + 16d) − (a + 9d) = 7 7d = 7 ⇒ d = 1 Check: (17 − 10) × 1 = 7

Exercise 5.2 Q11-Q12: compare terms without mixing up indices

Two worked questions. A difference in term values is not a difference in indices.

Exercise 5.2 Q11: 54th term se 132 zyada

AP hai: 3, 15, 27, 39, …, isliye d = 12. Required term, 54th term se 132 zyada hai: aₙ − a₅₄ = 132. Matlab 12 ke size wale 11 gaps, toh n = 65. Hamari check mein a₅₄ = 639 aur a₆₅ = 771; difference 132 hai. Answer 65th term hai, term ki value 65 nahin.

d = 15 − 3 = 12 aₙ − a₅₄ = (n − 54)d 132 = (n − 54) × 12 n − 54 = 11 ⇒ n = 65 a₅₄ = 3 + 53 × 12 = 639 a₆₅ = 3 + 64 × 12 = 771 771 − 639 = 132

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p62, Exercise 5.2 Q11. Hamari vyakhya, working, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

Exercise 5.2 Q12: Same d, corresponding terms ka fixed difference

Dono APs ka common difference same hai aur unke 100th terms ka difference 100 hai. First terms a aur b lo. Har same index n par (n − 1)d wale parts cancel, toh difference a − b rehta hai. Isliye 1000th terms ka difference bhi 100 hai, 1000 nahin. Sawaal nahin batata kis AP ke terms bade hain; 100 difference ka size hai.

Aₙ = a + (n − 1)d Bₙ = b + (n − 1)d Aₙ − Bₙ = a − b |A₁₀₀ − B₁₀₀| = |a − b| = 100 |A₁₀₀₀ − B₁₀₀₀| = |(a + 999d) − (b + 999d)| = |a − b| = 100

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p62, Exercise 5.2 Q12. Hamari vyakhya, working, extra checks aur translation; official answer key nahin.

प्रश्नावली 5.2 Q11: 54वें पद से 132 अधिक

AP है: 3, 15, 27, 39, …, इसलिए d = 12 है। माँगा गया पद 54वें पद से 132 अधिक है: aₙ − a₅₄ = 132। इसका अर्थ 12 के आकार वाले 11 अंतराल हैं, इसलिए n = 65 है। हमारी जाँच में a₅₄ = 639 और a₆₅ = 771 मिलते हैं; उनका अंतर 132 है। उत्तर 65वाँ पद है, पद का मान 65 नहीं।

d = 15 − 3 = 12 aₙ − a₅₄ = (n − 54)d 132 = (n − 54) × 12 n − 54 = 11 ⇒ n = 65 a₅₄ = 3 + 53 × 12 = 639 a₆₅ = 3 + 64 × 12 = 771 771 − 639 = 132

NCERT jemh105, Reprint 2026-27, p62, Exercise 5.2 Q11. हमारी व्याख्या, हल, अतिरिक्त जाँच और अनुवाद; आधिकारिक उत्तरमाला नहीं।

प्रश्नावली 5.2 Q12: समान d, संगत पदों का स्थिर अंतर

दो AP का सार्व अंतर समान है और उनके 100वें पदों का अंतर 100 है। उनके पहले पद a और b मानें। किसी भी समान स्थान n पर (n − 1)d वाले भाग कट जाते हैं, इसलिए अंतर a − b रहता है। अतः उनके 1000वें पदों का अंतर भी 100 है, 1000 नहीं। प्रश्न यह नहीं बताता कि किस AP के पद बड़े हैं; 100 अंतर का परिमाण है।

Aₙ = a + (n − 1)d Bₙ = b + (n − 1)d Aₙ − Bₙ = a − b |A₁₀₀ − B₁₀₀| = |a − b| = 100 |A₁₀₀₀ − B₁₀₀₀| = |(a + 999d) − (b + 999d)| = |a − b| = 100

Exercise 5.2 Q11: 132 more than the 54th term

The AP is 3, 15, 27, 39, …, so d = 12. The required term is 132 more than the 54th term: aₙ − a₅₄ = 132. This means 11 gaps of size 12, giving n = 65. Our check finds a₅₄ = 639 and a₆₅ = 771; their difference is 132. The answer is the 65th term, not the value 65.

d = 15 − 3 = 12 aₙ − a₅₄ = (n − 54)d 132 = (n − 54) × 12 n − 54 = 11 ⇒ n = 65 a₅₄ = 3 + 53 × 12 = 639 a₆₅ = 3 + 64 × 12 = 771 771 − 639 = 132

Exercise 5.2 Q12: Same d, constant corresponding-term difference

Two APs have the same common difference, and the difference between their 100th terms is 100. Let their first terms be a and b. At any common index n, the (n − 1)d terms cancel, so the difference is a − b. Therefore the difference between their 1000th terms is also 100, not 1000. The question does not say which AP has the larger terms; 100 is the size of the difference.

Aₙ = a + (n − 1)d Bₙ = b + (n − 1)d Aₙ − Bₙ = a − b |A₁₀₀ − B₁₀₀| = |a − b| = 100 |A₁₀₀₀ − B₁₀₀₀| = |(a + 999d) − (b + 999d)| = |a − b| = 100

Exercise 5.2 Q13-Q14: count numbers in a range with an AP

Two counting questions. Find the first and last terms inside the range, then count terms, watching for off-by-one errors.

Exercise 5.2 Q13: 7 se divisible three-digit numbers

7 se divisible three-digit numbers ek AP banati hain: 105, 112, 119, …, 994, jisme d = 7 hai. nth term ko last term ke barabar rakhne par 105 + (n − 1) × 7 = 994 se n = 128 milta hai. Hamari check: 105 = 15 × 7 aur 994 = 142 × 7, toh ye 7 ke 15th se 142nd multiples hain, matlab 142 − 15 + 1 = 128 numbers. Dono methods se wahi answer aata hai.

AP: 105, 112, …, 994; d = 7 aₙ = 105 + (n − 1) × 7 994 = 105 + (n − 1) × 7 n − 1 = 889 ÷ 7 = 127 ⇒ n = 128 Check: 105 = 15 × 7, 994 = 142 × 7 142 − 15 + 1 = 128

Exercise 5.2 Q13: Three-digit numbers divisible by 7

The three-digit numbers divisible by 7 form an AP: 105, 112, 119, …, 994, with d = 7. Setting the nth term equal to the last term, 105 + (n − 1) × 7 = 994, gives n = 128. Our check: 105 = 15 × 7 and 994 = 142 × 7, so they are the 15th to 142nd multiples of 7, which is 142 − 15 + 1 = 128 numbers. Both methods agree.

AP: 105, 112, …, 994; d = 7 aₙ = 105 + (n − 1) × 7 994 = 105 + (n − 1) × 7 n − 1 = 889 ÷ 7 = 127 ⇒ n = 128 Check: 105 = 15 × 7, 994 = 142 × 7 142 − 15 + 1 = 128

Exercise 5.2 Q14: Multiples of 4 between 10 and 250

The multiples of 4 between 10 and 250 are 12, 16, 20, …, 248, an AP with d = 4. From 12 + (n − 1) × 4 = 248 we get n = 60. Our check: 12 = 3 × 4 and 248 = 62 × 4, so these are the 3rd to 62nd multiples of 4, which is 62 − 3 + 1 = 60 multiples. Note that 10 and 250 are not multiples of 4, so the endpoints 12 and 248 are the nearest multiples inside the range.

AP: 12, 16, …, 248; d = 4 aₙ = 12 + (n − 1) × 4 248 = 12 + (n − 1) × 4 n − 1 = 236 ÷ 4 = 59 ⇒ n = 60 Check: 12 = 3 × 4, 248 = 62 × 4 62 − 3 + 1 = 60

Exercise 5.2 Q15-Q16: two APs and two clues

Two questions that turn words into nth-term equations. Set the two expressions equal, or read d straight out of a term difference.

Exercise 5.2 Q15: Do APs ke equal nth terms

63, 65, 67, … (d = 2) aur 3, 10, 17, … (d = 7) ke nth terms equal hone chahiye. 63 + (n − 1) × 2 = 3 + (n − 1) × 7 rakhne par 65 = 5n milta hai, toh n = 13. Hamari check: pehli AP ka 13th term 63 + 12 × 2 = 87 aur doosri ka 3 + 12 × 7 = 87; dono match karte hain, matlab 13th terms equal hain.

63 + (n − 1) × 2 = 3 + (n − 1) × 7 61 + 2n = 7n − 4 65 = 5n ⇒ n = 13 Check: a₁₃ = 63 + 12 × 2 = 87 b₁₃ = 3 + 12 × 7 = 87 87 = 87

Exercise 5.2 Q16: Do term clues se AP find karna

Teesra term 16 hai, toh a + 2d = 16. 7th term 5th term se 12 zyada hai, aur wo difference theek 2d hai, toh d = 6. Phir a = 16 − 2 × 6 = 4, jisse AP milti hai: 4, 10, 16, 22, … . Hamari check: 3rd term 4 + 2 × 6 = 16, aur 7th term 40 minus 5th term 28 = 12, jaise chahiye. Dhyan do, difference mein first terms cancel ho jaate hain, isliye d seedha nikal aata hai.

a + 2d = 16 (a + 6d) − (a + 4d) = 12 2d = 12 ⇒ d = 6 a = 16 − 12 = 4 AP: 4, 10, 16, 22, … Check: a₃ = 16, a₇ − a₅ = 40 − 28 = 12

Exercise 5.2 Q15: Equal nth terms of two APs

The nth terms of 63, 65, 67, … (d = 2) and 3, 10, 17, … (d = 7) must be equal. Setting 63 + (n − 1) × 2 = 3 + (n − 1) × 7 gives 65 = 5n, so n = 13. Our check: the 13th term of the first AP is 63 + 12 × 2 = 87, and of the second is 3 + 12 × 7 = 87; both match, so the 13th terms are equal.

63 + (n − 1) × 2 = 3 + (n − 1) × 7 61 + 2n = 7n − 4 65 = 5n ⇒ n = 13 Check: a₁₃ = 63 + 12 × 2 = 87 b₁₃ = 3 + 12 × 7 = 87 87 = 87

Exercise 5.2 Q16: Find the AP from two term clues

The third term is 16, so a + 2d = 16. The 7th term exceeds the 5th term by 12, and that difference is exactly 2d, so d = 6. Then a = 16 − 2 × 6 = 4, giving the AP 4, 10, 16, 22, … . Our check: the 3rd term is 4 + 2 × 6 = 16, and the 7th term 40 minus the 5th term 28 is 12, as required. Note how the first terms cancel in the difference, which is why d comes out directly.

a + 2d = 16 (a + 6d) − (a + 4d) = 12 2d = 12 ⇒ d = 6 a = 16 − 12 = 4 AP: 4, 10, 16, 22, … Check: a₃ = 16, a₇ − a₅ = 40 − 28 = 12

Exercise 5.2 Q18: First three terms from two sums

Exercise 5.2 Q19-Q20: APs in salary and savings

Two word problems. Turn yearly salary and weekly savings into an AP, and mind which year or week the index points to.

Exercise 5.2 Q19: Salary ₹7000 tak pahunchna

Subba Rao ne 1995 mein ₹5000 salary par kaam shuru kiya aur har saal ₹200 ka increment mila, toh unki yearly salaries a = 5000 aur d = 200 wali ek AP banati hain. 5000 + (n − 1) × 200 = 7000 rakhne par n = 11 milta hai, matlab kaam ka 11th year, jo 1995 + 10 = 2005 hai. Hamari check: 2005 tak 10 increments poori hoti hain, aur 5000 + 10 × 200 = 7000 exact aata hai. Answer saal 2005 hai, 2006 nahin; 1995 khud pehla saal hai aur usme koi increment nahin milta.

a = 5000, d = 200 5000 + (n − 1) × 200 = 7000 (n − 1) × 200 = 2000 n − 1 = 10 ⇒ n = 11 Year = 1995 + 10 = 2005 Check: 5000 + 10 × 200 = 7000
